# Forever Suite in game testing

## Clicked group buff requests — 0.2.85

October 19: user reports clicking Arcane Intellect did not announce the missing buff. This authorizes deliberate incoming-icon chat requests, replacing the reminder-only left-click behavior from 0.2.84. No automatic announcements or live messages are sent by development tools.

Record actual /wfs status build/Interface, class, group type and active addons. Native chat delivery is pending; the previous click report confirms lack of announcement in 0.2.84, not delivery of this correction.

1. /reload into **0.2.85**. On the unbuffed Hunter grouped with a nearby Mage, left-click the gold Arcane Intellect icon. Expect one “Could I get Arcane Intellect, please?” in party chat. Verify it appears to the other player, rather than relying only on a local echo. No spell, item or target action should occur. Tooltip should explain click-to-request and cooldown.
2. Reclick immediately: no second request; local feedback explains the cooldown. Another family is limited by three seconds globally, the same family by thirty seconds. After those intervals, another deliberate click may request again. Refresh/aura events and waiting must never send automatically. Right-click still hides a family without chat. Own usable spell/item icons must retain their original actions.
3. Test a home raid and instance group; use RAID and INSTANCE_CHAT respectively, with instance grouping preferred when both exist. No whisper, SAY or alternate-channel fallback. Check a disbanded group, departed provider, buff applied just before clicking, unavailable data, disabled module/incoming option and combat: no stale request or queued retry. Native restriction/failure should give local feedback.
4. With expiry reminders on, click a due incoming buff: wording requests it refreshed. Once the buff is adequately covered, a stale click must not request it. Check localized names, long-name bounds and usual addons for Lua/blocked-action/taint behavior. No profile setting, ownership or other addon data should change.

Offline: **60 Lua 5.1 files, identical 0.2.85 TOCs and 14,071 substitute checks pass** (67 above 0.2.84). Fake senders validate dispatch and rejection boundaries, not server delivery. Native results remain pending.

Delivery: dist/WoWForeverSuite-0.2.85.zip contains 68 byte-identical source files. SHA-256 ad95a5f0de283269ad3231982c6fd88fcfd4b6cd61cd21d5acb5a61cb88220e2. No deployment, junction change or remote publication.

## Class-aware buff reminders and controls — 0.2.84

October 19: user requests recommendation #8 and reports missing Arcane Intellect never appeared on a Hunter grouped with a Mage. Previous routing deliberately sent incoming families only to local chat; a positive-only spell-level predicate also rejected legal zero values, and resting suppressed the whole ordinary bar. These are identified suppression paths; no native trace establishes which affected the reported encounter.

Installed executable **1.60.1.70450**; record actual /wfs status build/Interface, language, UI scale, class/level, group composition and active addons. Native results for 0.2.84 remain pending. Prior standalone Profiles presentation is user-confirmed; full profile persistence/security results remain unreported.

1. Priority reproduction: /reload into **0.2.84**, enable **Missing buff bar** under **Combat → Missing Buffs** if off, and keep **Buffs my group can give me** enabled. On an unbuffed Hunter, group with a nearby living connected Mage outside combat. A gold **Group** Arcane Intellect icon should persist; hover identifies the possible provider and explains it cannot cast for them. Click must not cast, retarget, send chat or open an item action. Let the Mage buff you: icon should disappear; remove/expire the buff and verify it returns. Check higher/lower ranks and Brilliance. Repeat in a rested area: default reminders remain visible there.
2. Right-click that incoming icon to hide the family. Open **Manage buff reminders**, search Intellect or Mage, and click **Show reminder**. Only that family should restore; unrelated hidden families/poisons stay as chosen. Test All/Hidden/My class filters, localized alternate names, multiple pages, no results and reload persistence. Test the incoming/self/outgoing/upkeep switches independently; no switch should implicitly enable another.
3. Repeat Intellect on all Mana classes, especially Druid while shifted. Rogue/Warrior should be excluded by the default class-aware filter; turning Class-aware targets off intentionally permits all. On a Mage, verify outgoing targets include Hunter/Druid but skip non-Mana classes by default. Existing aura data, absent/hidden/dead/disconnected providers and known missing metadata must behave conservatively. Record actual readings if the expected candidate is absent; do not treat inaccessible data as missing.
4. Priest: Fortitude, Spirit at level 30, Fear Ward at 20 and Shadow Protection when the native learned-level metadata permits it. Druid: Mark/Thorns. Warrior: appropriate shout coverage. Paladin: useful Might/Wisdom alternatives and existing Kings/other relevant coverage; a conflicting/irrelevant alternative should not mask a useful missing buff. When providing buffs automatically select among learned alternatives per recipient; explicit choices remain respected. Do not demand all mutually exclusive blessings or infer an unobserved Hunter talent. Verify ordinary party auras versus Forever resistance aura coverage across raid subgroups, range and real spell eligibility.
5. Open **Custom learned buff spells**. Search by localized name, select an actual icon/name, add a reminder without typing an ID, switch Self/Party-raid, and remove it. Built-in results should open the family manager. Highest learned ranks should update naturally; duplicate ranks cannot consume extra slots. Check eight custom entries, both columns, long names, multiple spell-result pages, empty results and asynchronous learned-spell data. No cast should happen from selecting/adding/browsing. Existing saved IDs/hidden choices must remain usable.
6. Open **Buff bar appearance**. Cycle bar scales and icon sizes; inspect live bar, tooltip anchors, title centering, eight-icon row wrap, preview, small/large UI scale and every custom-list/footer control. Lock movement: dragging/header Ctrl-right-click must not change position; unlock restores normal drag. Deliberate settings reset remains available. Move, /reload and relog to verify normalized position and display preferences. Class-aware and rested-area choices should persist through named/shared/personal settings profiles without resetting other tools or data.
7. Enter combat during drag, preview, a custom-spell selection or metadata load. Ordinary bar must hide via its secure controller, including incoming icons. No secure action, position, scale or size mutation is allowed in combat; controls disable and deferred opening cancels on navigation/close. Existing optional passive combat reminders remain separate, use only permitted data, and do not promise incoming coverage in combat. Leave combat: visible state should recompute without a stale cast or manager reopen. Disable the feature and close settings: no idle/retry worker or automatic group message should remain.
8. Regression: self/first-recipient casting, native range/reagents, wrong/stale target rejection, alternative armor/aspect equivalence, active aura toggle protection, warning thresholds, carried coating/poison selection, missing supply notices, macro ownership and no automatic item use. Repeat with the usual addon setup and record actual Lua/blocked-action/taint results. Existing profiles, gear weights, inventory, training and other addon/SavedVariables data must remain intact.

Offline: **60 Lua 5.1 files, identical 0.2.84 TOCs and 14,004 substitute checks pass** (264 above 0.2.83). Cursor-driven scroll checks and all existing behavior checks remain. Layout models use approximate fonts and are not native screenshots. Native class coverage, secure casting, persistence, rendering and taint safety remain unverified.

Delivery: dist/WoWForeverSuite-0.2.84.zip contains 68 byte-identical source files. SHA-256 ef1bff46d69e457ec007bdc8d7dd65bf46403dfa48e15a3dbe3587576b73e6ea. No deployment, junction modification or remote publication.

## Settings and layout profiles — 0.2.82

October 19: installed WowB.exe **1.60.1.70450**. Record /wfs status, actual running build/Interface, character, language, UI scale and active addons. All native profile results remain pending; substitute checks do not establish persistence or taint safety.

1. Before updating, note enabled tools, supported window positions/sizes, meter layouts, custom buff choices, gear weights, leveling position and saved inventory/macros. /reload into **0.2.82**. Confirm everything remains as configured, Default is active/shared, no new optional tool turns on, and /wfs still works if the minimap button was hidden. Fresh characters should inherit Default; a fresh account should retain the normal off defaults and visible launcher without editing.
2. Open **Home → Profiles** and **/wfs profiles**. Check the active profile/scope caption on other pages. Enter a unique name and save a copy. It should appear selected without activation. Rename it; verify whitespace/duplicate/long/formatted/empty name feedback. Fill twelve slots and test profile pagination. Default and assigned/active profiles must not be removable.
3. Review a different profile. Check listed enabled/disabled settings and layouts, especially vendor, quest automation, inventory capture and macro-update choices. Cancel and navigate away; no changes/reload should happen. Confirm **Use & reload** and verify target choices after native reload; old profile must retain its outgoing settings. Repeat with many changes, long names and alternate UI scales; all review text and final actions must scroll clear of the fixed footer.
4. On two characters, select the same shared profile. Change ordinary switches, meter appearance and supported window geometry; log out/reload. Verify shared settings on the other character. On one, choose **Only this character**, review/reload, then change one setting and a nested layout. Verify the other character keeps the base values. Change an untouched shared setting on the other character; personal character should inherit that setting on its next login while retaining its private differences. Entire changed layout/choice tables are personal units.
5. Choose **Use shared settings**, review/reload, and confirm private differences are suspended without publishing them. Return to personal scope and confirm private changes resume. Switch between two named profiles with different personal overrides; each should retain its own differences. Test /reload and a complete logout/login, including a character that has not previously used this update.
6. Visit both characters after the update. Choose a source in the paged character picker, enter a new name and save a copy. Confirm last-saved source values, independent future changes, persistence, readable source labels and popup dismissal. Recorded inventory is not required. Old character roster entries alone do not create settings sources until that character runs this update.
7. Delete an unused profile with confirmation; cancel once first. Verify only that profile disappears. A profile still assigned to another recorded character must be protected. Verify missing/corrupt selection falls back to Default in an isolated disposable test database; do not alter the user's actual SavedVariables to conduct this check.
8. Enter combat with a review/picker open; actions must disable and the picker close. Leave combat: no switch or reload should fire automatically. Open Profiles for the first time in combat, close/navigate away, and leave combat; no stale reopen should occur. Check no Lua errors or blocked actions with the usual addon setup.
9. Switch across profiles with window tools, meter/bar positions, castbar sizes, chat and native progress text/bag-order options enabled versus disabled. Verify native restoration markers restore the correct prior preference, positions fit the screen, and unsupported/native Edit Mode frames remain governed by the game. Leveling position/session, gear profiles/weights, training records, inventory/banks/favorites, macro contents/ownership, mail contacts, safety blacklist and guide completion must remain intact. No macro, item transaction or inventory scan should happen from merely browsing/copying a profile.

Offline: **59 Lua 5.1 files, identical 0.2.82 TOCs and 13,805 substitute checks pass**, 287 above 0.2.81. Includes real startup/event wiring, deep-copy reload simulations, two-character inheritance, data boundaries, native restore markers, safe failure paths, limits/corruption/restricted values, combat and UI pagination/review bounds. Native rendering, reload permissions, persistence and taint remain unverified.

## Classic class macro recipes — 0.2.80

October 19: user approves the wider GUI and asks for researched macros. Installed WowB.exe **1.60.1.70450**; record actual running build/Interface, class/level, language and active addons. Macro execution on the Forever beta remains unverified.

1. /reload and confirm **0.2.80**. Open /wfs macros on each Classic class: five General recipes plus exactly two matching class recipes should precede custom item choices. Other classes' sections must stay hidden. Browsing, collapsing and searching for a class spell must never create a game macro or enable updates. Preserve existing Hunter pair, pet-food choices and manual/custom macros.
2. Preview each available spell, click Create and drag the resulting General macro onto the action bar. Confirm no configuration/rank/equipment edits are needed. Test learned and unlearned abilities, metadata loading and a non-English client if available. Generated localized names should work with normal highest-rank selection; missing data must leave existing text intact. Native name/body limits and full General slots must fail safely.
3. Druid: Innervate living friendly mouseover → friendly target → self; Rebirth dead friendly mouseover → dead friendly target. No self-resurrection or explicit form cancellation. Mage: Counterspell stops a current cast and uses enemy mouseover/target; Polymorph respects living/hostile and creature restrictions. Paladin: Hammer of Justice stops a cast on the current enemy; Freedom uses living friendly mouseover/target/self.
4. Priest: Shield uses living friendly mouseover/target/self; Dispel supports a living allied/enemy mouseover, then target, then self only when native spell rules allow it. Rogue: Kick and Blind use living enemy mouseover/target without changing selected target; do not assume combo-point behavior. Shaman: Earth Shock stops a cast and uses the highest learned rank on mouseover enemy/target; Chain Heal uses friendly mouseover/target/self.
5. Warlock: Pet recall makes the pet Passive and Follow, including during combat; pet remains Passive afterward. Drain Life must not restart its own or another active channel; after it ends, a fresh press casts normally. Warrior: Heroic Strike queues and begins current-enemy autoattacks; repeated presses should not deliberately unqueue it. Taunt uses enemy mouseover/target and obeys the client's stance rules. Check out-of-range, dead/invalid/missing targets, no pet, no resource and native reagent requirements without automatic fallback after a valid-but-unusable target.
6. Create only chosen recipes. Rename/create a conflicting General macro and manually edit an owned body: Suite must preserve it. Reload/relog and switch to an alt of another class: inactive records remain but cannot rewrite foreign macros. Enable updates only if desired; only previously created current-class owned macros update. Combat/native-editor-open states must block writes; leaving the page cancels pending presentation without changing active macros. Check native errors, blocked actions and taint with the usual addons.

Offline: **57 Lua 5.1 files, identical 0.2.80 TOCs and 13,517 substitute checks pass** (574 above 0.2.79). Native spell availability, secure macro execution/targeting, localization, persistence and taint remain pending in-game tests. API substitutes verify generated text and writer behavior, not native spell IDs/availability, targeting/secure execution or persistent disk writes.

Delivery: `dist/WoWForeverSuite-0.2.80.zip` contains **65 byte-identical addon source files**, SHA-256 `662b5944c33fdfe277a15f880f924b40b05a8358909743cab9e47ff6d62abe8c`. The game junction remains pointed at canonical P-drive source. No deployment, junction changes or remote publication.

## Scrollbar gesture control — 0.2.78

October 19 native evidence: video shows **0.2.77** with jumping long thumbs on Action Bars/Other Bars; user reports Gear Details improved. This validates the need for a gesture correction, not the new code. Installed WowB.exe **1.60.1.70450**; record actual running build/Interface, UI scale and addon setup on retest.

1. /reload and confirm **0.2.78**. Repeat the recording's gestures on Overview, Action Bars and Other Bars. Grab the center, then near the top and bottom of a long thumb. Press without moving: neither thumb nor content should jump. Move a few pixels down/up: thumb must follow that distance continuously in the same direction. Reverse before releasing, drag outside the track, release outside the GUI, then return: no continued motion or snap-back.
2. Wheel down/up over the content, track and thumb. All must move the same viewport consistently. Click empty track below/above the thumb: page down/up without recentering the thumb under the pointer. Check top/middle/bottom, short pages with no bar, full expanded Gear Details/custom editor and all 24 settings pages.
3. Test macro recipe list, macro text preview and setup review independently: their gestures must leave the outer page still. Collapse groups, select shorter/longer content, change tabs or close while dragging. No old drag may continue after range/page changes or reopening. Setup scrolling must not apply choices.
4. Repeat at normal/alternate UI scales. Large thumbs should retain their grab point and represent viewport size. Enter combat during a drag: pointer tracking must stop; geometry waits until combat ends. Wheel movement may update content while thumb geometry is deferred. After combat, thumb catches up without reopening hidden UI. Check errors, blocked actions and taint with the usual addon setup.

## Character upgrade profiles and explained scores — 0.2.75

October 19: user requests professional-quality upgrade comparisons and defaults that work without manual editing. Installed executable re-read as **1.60.1.70450**. Record /wfs status, the actual running build/Interface, class/level, client language, UI scale and active addons. Native results remain pending.

1. /reload and confirm **0.2.75**. Open **/wfs gear** (or Bags & Gear → Gear Details). Existing characters must retain their old role and Stats/Item level choice; a new character starts Automatic/Stats. Possible upgrades retains its existing enable state and starts off for a new account. Enable it if needed. Use Comparison profile to choose Automatic or a ready-made role; no weight editing should be necessary. Check Mage/Warlock/Priest Caster starters, Hunter ranged-DPS emphasis, Rogue/Warrior melee-DPS emphasis and the disclosed Balanced fallback for hybrids. Judge practical usefulness on actual gear; do not mark presets calibrated from substitute fixtures.
2. Hover wearable upgrades and compare the teal + with the tooltip's named profile, equipped/candidate totals and positive/negative stat changes. Check a tradeoff (gain one stat, lose another), a tie, a downgrade, future-level/unusable gear, uncached gear and a known empty armor slot. Empty-slot text must give the reason without a fabricated score. Check tiny positive custom-weight gains do not say +0.0. Repeat with icon item levels disabled, inventory counts enabled and comparison tooltips; no duplicate annotation or optional-method error should occur.
3. Test two different rings/trinkets: Full breakdown must identify the weaker actual item being replaced. Test main/off-hand weapons, dual wield when learned, a two-hander replacing a complete pair, a held off-hand and ranged gear. Compare all signed contribution changes to the shown score difference. Confirm the 50% off-hand base-DPS assumption and the unmodeled speed/proc/talent/set effects are disclosed. Do not treat the largest score as a guaranteed best configuration.
4. Open Details & custom weights. Built-in values must be read-only. Create a custom copy; give it a distinct name and change an integer, a decimal and a zero weight. Typing must not change existing hints. Save & use must apply the complete valid model; Cancel, closing settings and navigating away must discard unfinished edits. ## Follow Windows audio device — 0.2.31

Installed executable re-read October 14: 1.60.1.70374. Record `/wfs status`, actual running build/Interface, normal audio settings and active addons. Disable other addons' overlapping audio-sync features for the isolated test. This is an output-device feature; Windows default playback and default communications devices can differ. Change the default **playback** device for the core test.

1. `/reload`, confirm 0.2.31, then `/wfs audio` or **Everyday Tools → Audio**. Follow Windows audio device starts off. Verify both rows/help/footer fit and settings search for `windows headphones sync` finds it. With it off, test that native output selection stays under your control and the Suite does no device work. Enable: native Options → Audio → Game Sound Output should select System Default. Volume, mute, background sound, microphone and voice-chat output selections should remain as previously set.
2. Play ordinary game music/ambient sound. While WoW remains running, switch Windows default playback from speakers to headphones/headset and back; allow at least two seconds between changes. Return focus to WoW before judging sound if native Sound in Background is off. Audio should move to the newly selected output without `/reload` or a game restart, with at most a brief refresh interruption. Test unplug/replug and a newly connected device where available. Confirm actual sound, not just the dropdown value. Repeated device notifications should settle without ongoing cuts/restarts, Lua errors or blocked actions.
3. Choose **Sync now** while enabled; it should request one refresh, including when an event was missed. Disabled Sync now should explain enabling without changing sound. Test changing native output to a fixed device with sync disabled; it must stay fixed. Re-enable to select System Default again. Very rapid changes fall inside the two-second feedback throttle; wait and use Sync now to check the final route if needed. This behavior must not be mistaken for idle polling or an endless recovery loop.
4. During an available cinematic/movie, change Windows output or click Sync now. Do not skip it for the test: Suite should wait until playback ends before refreshing, preserving its audio. Test enabling while a movie is already visible, queued refresh just before playback starts, and disabling before it ends. Stale work must not run later. Naturally unknown/unavailable media data must pause rather than guess; only observed cases can be recorded as native results.
5. With sync already enabled, switch output during combat and check native game sound follows without protected UI work/taint. First enable in combat should wait for event-frame creation after combat; disabling before exit should cancel it. Queue a device event and disable/logout before the worker runs: no stale refresh should follow. Reload/relog/restart to verify the independently saved toggle and successful routing after login. No other addon's settings or SavedVariables should change.
6. Test with game sound muted and with altered volume sliders, plus a voice-chat output deliberately different from game output. Sync should preserve those choices. Re-enable the normal addon setup after the isolated run and repeat a speaker/headset switch. Record exact Windows playback choices, observed audible route, CVar/refresh rejection feedback, cinema/combat timing and any errors. The legacy restart capability's presence or a nonthrowing call alone does not establish routing/ordinary-addon permission.

With three free regular slots the central button must be hidden. Fill to two, one and zero; it must appear at the exact threshold with an accurate count. Free a third slot: it hides. Repeat using individual/combined bags, bag replacement and a specialty/reagent bag with many free slots; specialized slots must not suppress regular-bag pressure. Opening a bank must not count its slots or offer its contents.
2. Carry gray and white expendable items with different vendor prices and stack sizes, plus green-or-better, locked, quest/quest-starting and no-vendor-value items such as Hearthstone. Only eligible gray/white stacks may be offered. Compare the **whole stack** vendor value, rather than unit price. Check the icon, long name/count, quality text color, gold/silver/copper amount and tooltip at normal and changed UI scales. No item should move or be deleted solely because the alert appears or bags change. With every eligible item blacklisted or excluded, keep a compact no-eligible-items alert; clicking it does nothing.
3. Right-click an offer: that item type and all its stacks must disappear from suggestions, and the next cheapest type must appear. Reload, relog, restart and log into another character to confirm the shared blacklist and toggle persist. Use **Clear trash blacklist** and verify only these protections reset; vendor selling, sorting, recorded inventory and other preferences stay intact.
4. Left-click an expendable offer: it moves to the cursor and the **native DELETE_ITEM** dialog must remain visible, naming the exact item; the central alert hides. Cancel/Escape must return the entire stack and restore the offer. Reopen and manually Accept: only the offered stack may be deleted, then the next candidate appears while slots remain low. Record any blocked-action/Lua error or missing prompt from **ConfirmDeleteItem**; source HasRestrictions and substitutes do not prove live permission. A denied/unavailable request must not delete, auto-confirm or retry the item.
5. Sort, move, replace, split or change the offered stack before clicking; clicks must revalidate instead of acting on a replacement. Acquire a cheaper eligible stack before a queued refresh, or free a third slot: an obsolete offer cannot be prepared. Hold an item, spell, macro or money on the cursor and click; it must stay untouched. Leave another native dialog open; the alert cannot replace it. ## First-login setup guide — 0.1.98

Installed executable re-read October 11: **1.60.1.70338**; running GetBuildInfo()/Interface remain uncollected. Record `/wfs status`, enabled overlapping addons and UI scale. The existing addon junction targets this workspace. Do not delete or externally edit SavedVariables for this test.

It must not change gameplay or styles by opening. Change Auto Loot later in game Options; reload/relog/restart must not force the setup choice again. If the setting is unavailable or changes permissions, its card must show N/A, or Apply must retain the draft with an explanation. Use current choices on Everyday to leave that setting alone and finish other choices.
5. Enter combat while reviewing staged changes. Apply becomes unavailable, and combat exit must not apply by itself. Click afterward to finish. Enter combat during first deferred opening if practical; leaving combat opens the requested guide once, while navigating away/closing settings cancels the pending opening. Existing controls may edit drafts in combat but must not move/scale native protected frames, write Auto Loot or activate new features until explicit finish outside combat. 6. After applying window tools, move a bag and several standard windows from empty headers; native title menus, close buttons and other header controls must work. Ctrl+scroll resizes and Ctrl+right-click resets outside combat. Arrange native bars/minimap in Edit Mode, and test selected styles against normal actions/controls. Try recorded inventory/bank/mail features only after alt logins/bank visits; normal sending and bank actions stay manual. Use each feature's existing checklist for its detailed behavior.
7. Reload, relog and restart after completion. Chosen preferences and one-time welcome state must persist. Rerunning setup starts from current settings, and normal settings remain independently editable. Confirm `/wfs` and `/wfs setup` still work with the minimap button hidden. Verify clipboard contents instead of inferring success from highlights. Click the glyph again for normal links/click-through; hiding its toolbar should also end selection.

Drag it to the bottom left, resize, save/exit, type and confirm the bottom border stays on screen. Repeat at alternate UI scale and after `/reload`/relog, retaining the native drag/resize/save workflow. Check headers/prompts, short/long drafts and autocomplete; native height and sending/focus must stay intact. Switch General/Combat Log and custom tabs, use overflow, dock/undock/minimize/close/reopen, and open a native temporary whisper window. Alignment and defaults should follow without forcing hidden windows shown. Preserve input callbacks, alerts, scrollbar and return-to-current. Copy in another window: the old one returns to normal. Hiding or typing ends selection. Sending, autocomplete, drafts, menus and modifier clicks must remain native.
4. Change size/opacity, `/reload`, relog and restart. Existing alts and a newly created character should inherit enabled account defaults. Restore Blizzard chat: original typing-box anchors/width, native Edit Mode selection/clamp, native/current fonts, decoration alpha and links/input return, and the glyph hides, retaining the two defaults. Re-enable and repeat with a moved/resized chat window. Native font/opacity resets while enabled settle to Suite defaults; restoration honors the latest native settings. No Suite chat history or another addon's settings is written.

Selection ends on an eligible unprotected frame; latest style/restoration applies afterward. Watch Lua errors, blocked actions and taint, including whispers/Combat Log with restricted combat messages. ## First test

1. Restart the client if needed, enable **Forever Suite**, log in and enter `/wfs status`. Record the version, build and Interface number. The installed executable re-read October 6 is 1.60.1.70245; refreshed UI source matches that build, with addon sources identical to the earlier 70205/70235 cache. The running API result is the final authority.
2. Enter `/console scriptErrors 1` to show Lua errors during testing, and open `/wfs`. Check the taller panel and two-line helpers fit at your UI scale. Overview must show move/resize/reset gestures without visiting Windows. Check that text is readable, each sidebar page opens, and both Escape and the top-right X close the panel. Reopen after clicking X and verify the header still drags. Move the panel, `/reload`, and verify its saved position.
3. Test one feature at a time. Disable another addon's corresponding feature first to avoid two addons changing the same frame.
4. Enable square shape and the stats row. Check the slim header sits just above the map and its centered area name, smaller centered subzone label, tracking, clock and calendar controls work. Enter and leave an inn, change zones, and verify both labels refresh. Header and stats backgrounds should be translucent with matching teal borders. Header left/right edges must align with the minimap outer border, including after changing map size or Edit Mode scale. The enlarged clock should sit just inside the map at bottom center and remain clickable. Long names must show in full, fitting or wrapping without covering controls; the header can grow for long labels and should shrink again for short names. Toggle square mode off and verify original clock font and anchors restore. Blizzard coordinates must be unobstructed above the stats row; its coordinate setting must remain unchanged. Toggle native coordinates/time to verify Suite fallback values appear only when needed. Check all stats toggles, and confirm an empty row hides. Check zoom, tracking, dungeon controls, rotated minimap, different UI scales and Edit Mode. With square mode enabled, enter Edit Mode and move the map closer to the top-right screen edge, allowing excess cluster space off screen. Save the native layout, reopen Edit Mode and reload to check persistence. Disable square mode and verify native screen clamping restores. Turn the options off and confirm the original shape and decorations return.
5. Enable window tools. Drag empty native header space without a separate drag bar. Test Character, Spellbook or Player Spells, Map, combined and individual Bags, Calendar, Bank, Vendor, Quest, Mail, Trade, Friends, Inspect, Auction, Trainer, Professions, Game Menu and Settings as each is available. Every native X must still close its window. Calendar's month title and empty side bars should drag; both arrows and Filters must work. Drag bag titles to move them; a plain left-click must open the title menu without moving the bag. Portrait settings must still open directly. A drag must not open the menu. Hold Ctrl and scroll over the header: up enlarges, down shrinks. Plain scrolling must not resize, and no corner icon should exist. Repeat native control clicks after resizing. Close and reopen; `/reload`; Ctrl-right-click the draggable header or background to reset each; reset all. Plain right-click and Ctrl-left-click must preserve saved layouts. Open and close other bags to verify native bag reflow preserves saved positions and sizes. If dragging fails, run `/wfs windows` with that window open and include its reported region count and input layer.
6. Start combat with a window already open. Try an option change, then finish combat. Check for blocked actions, frame jumps and errors. Also enter combat during an active move or resize. A normal out-of-combat test does not establish taint safety.
7. Enable icon and tooltip item levels. Test bags, equipment, vendor, bank, mail, quest rewards, loot and professions, including a newly encountered uncached item and an empty slot. Equipment and weapons should receive levels even if this character cannot use them; food, potions, crafting reagents, quest items, bags, quivers and ammunition must have no Suite level on their icons or tooltips. Reopen the Slitherskin Mackerel cooking recipe: neither its result nor the raw-fish reagent should show an item level. Then select a recipe that creates equipment and confirm that its gear result can show a level while its reagents remain unlabeled. Hold Shift over equipment to show comparison tooltips; confirm levels display without errors, duplicate annotations or stale numbers. Switch between gear and non-gear, scroll pooled lists, and close/reopen windows to confirm recycled buttons clear old levels. Repeat with icon/tooltip options independently enabled and with upgrade hints enabled. Record `/wfs status` and the actual client result.
8. Select a stat preset matching the intended role. Compare higher and lower primary-stat items, rings and empty equipment slots. A higher item level with a lower score must not be marked as better. Weapons and items whose benefit comes from special effects require manual judgment.
9. Enable player equipment levels and class name colors. Hover yourself and nearby players, including a previously unseen player while having no target selected. Keep hovering until inspect data arrives: clicking or targeting must not be required. Hover a different unseen player immediately afterward and hold through the inspect cooldown; it must load automatically. Move between players quickly, open the native Inspect window, go out of range and test during combat. Leave combat while still hovering to check automatic resumption. Confirm one gold Item Level label on the left and a white whole-number value aligned on the right, without the old sentence or item count. Repeated hovering and arriving inspect data must retain one row and the correct label/value. Confirm names and item levels belong to the current player, and that unavailable inspect data does not produce an invented number. Record the delay on fresh versus cached players, together with the running build.
10. Change a preference, `/reload`, log out and back in, then fully exit and restart the client. Confirm both preferences and layouts persist. `/wfs status` reports whether the SavedVariables table was present at startup; that alone is not a full persistence test.

## Vendor tools — 0.1.42

Record `/wfs status` and actual running build/Interface. The installed WowB.exe was re-read October 7 as 1.60.1.70245, matching the cached source; no running GetBuildInfo or live merchant results were captured during implementation. Disable overlapping vendor automation for these tests without changing its SavedVariables.

1. `/reload`, open `/wfs vendor` or **Vendor** in `/wfs`. Automatic repair and junk selling must start off independently; funding defaults to Personal gold and sale mode to Slow. Check the tenth sidebar entry, five rows, recovery explanation and note at normal and alternate UI scales. Flip funding and sale mode in both directions; Instant must visibly say items aren't recoverable. X, Escape and other pages must work, including reused settings rows after the passive recovery explanation. Visit a merchant with both tools off: no sale, repair or warning should occur.
2. With damaged gear, enable repair only and choose Personal. Visit a repair vendor: only your gold should fund one repair. A non-repair vendor must perform no repair. Test undamaged gear and insufficient money. With both tools enabled, an initially unaffordable repair should retry if sale proceeds cover it. Record the actual cost, balances, item durability, native error messages and whether all gear was repaired; the addon deliberately does not infer successful repairs from a return value.
3. Choose Guild and test with repair permission, enough bank money and enough remaining allowance. Confirm actual guild/personal balance changes. Repeat without permission, without a guild, with insufficient guild balance, insufficient allowance, and unlimited guild-leader allowance where available. Unavailable guild coverage should produce a skip message and no deliberate personal fallback. Test freshly loading guild balances and changed rank/funding data while the merchant is open. Server behavior and stale balance races must be recorded separately from local preflight checks.
4. Carry several expendable gray stacks plus ordinary white/green gear, a valueless item and items in a bag/backpack excluded using Blizzard's native junk setting. Enable junk only in Slow mode: one eligible stack should sell about every 0.2 seconds or more, appearing in native Buyback. Buy a sold stack back after the batch finishes; it must remain in bags during the same active visit. Carry more than 12 eligible stacks: stop after 12, report the limit and leave the rest in bags. Recover some, then test another deliberate visit and manual sales to observe native Buyback eviction. Recovery is limited to retained entries, not permanent. Check money totals and stack counts without risking valuable items.
5. Close the vendor mid-batch, move out of range, enter combat, change sale mode, and disable junk selling mid-batch. No stale timer may use the next item away from a merchant. Combat must stop sale work and require an open merchant to resume. Sort/move/replace queued items: changed ID/counts and new non-gray replacements must stay untouched. Try a cursor-held item and the native single-item repair cursor. Check slow mode never uses consumables, opens an item or accidentally repairs a bag item. Locked or unknown-data stacks may be skipped conservatively. Verify no Lua errors, blocked actions or ongoing timers with the merchant closed or tools disabled.
6. Switch to Instant with expendable junk and automatic junk selling enabled. Settings must explicitly warn that permanent sales aren't recoverable and need no confirmation. Visit a vendor: the native bulk sale should proceed automatically without a Suite popup. Check native Buyback contents, more than 12 stacks and excluded bags. While still at the vendor, acquire another expendable gray item and press Blizzard's Sell Junk button: its exact junk-sale popup should be automatically accepted and dismissed. Repeat with automatic selling disabled, repair only, and Slow selected: the native popup must retain its ordinary confirmation. Check other generic confirmation dialogs remain open and perform no action automatically. Close the vendor, enter combat, change mode or disable junk selling before the initial worker: no stale queued sale may run. An unsold visit may resume outside combat; an already completed automatic permanent sale must not repeat just because combat ends.
7. Enable repair and junk in each combination, including guild funding with automatic permanent sales. Check the two features remain independent, repeated merchant/guild/bag events don't duplicate requests, and other Suite settings don't restart a completed batch or reset its limit. Reload, relog and restart the client to verify the four account-wide preferences. Record actual build, API availability, repair/funds, sale/recovery, native-popup acceptance, visuals, input, taint and persistence before calling the feature verified.

## Reporting a problem

Record the client build, enabled Suite features, the window or item involved, the exact action that produced the problem and the Lua error text. For a layout problem, describe where it was and where it moved. Test with the overlapping feature in another addon disabled before attributing the behavior to this suite.

## Camping smoke test (0.1.23)

Record `/wfs status` and the actual running build/Interface with every result. The installed executable observation remains 1.60.1.70235; refreshed source metadata records 70235 with addon sources unchanged from 70205. Disable EverCore's overlapping camping bar for this test.

1. `/reload`, open `/wfs camping` and verify the seventh sidebar page, all four settings rows and footer fit. Camping starts off on first use. Enable the bar away from a camp: gameplay buttons must remain hidden. Preview position must show only an inactive outline; clicking its background must not activate anything behind it. Drag the preview header, close settings and reopen; check the saved placement. Preview must end on changing pages, closing with X/Escape or entering combat. Reset must affect only camping.
2. Approach a camp with the **Campfire Nearby** buff shown in the screenshot. Check automatic display even with no camp objects, then walk away and confirm it hides when that proximity buff ends. Keep an already earned camping benefit active away from the fire: it must not keep the bar visible. Record the actual proximity aura's spell ID if detection fails, along with its native tooltip.
3. Carry Lodestone and other finished camp objects, including duplicate stacks, a camp blueprint, materials and ordinary consumables. Each finished nearby-fire use object must appear exactly once with the combined carried count; ingredients/recipes/unrelated items must remain absent. Acquire a newly loaded object, split/move stacks, use the last object and remove an object from your bags. Actions, counts and tooltips must follow the current inventory, without stale buttons or errors. Check an item requiring a profession you lack: native feedback must retain that restriction. If more than eight distinct objects are available, verify wrapping keeps all of them accessible.
4. Hover an object for its normal tooltip and click to place it. Check the native targeting cursor/placement confirmation, profession requirement, campsite capacity and shared cooldown. Never infer a successful placement from the click alone. The native cooldown swipe must appear, with counts readable above it. Test native item tooltips/other Suite tooltip features for duplicates and errors. Record any object omitted despite carrying it and its full requirement tooltip. English tooltip discovery is the current scope.
5. Click Sit. Use the arrow to choose Kneel, Sleep and Lie down; selection must perform no emote until the pose button is clicked. Verify the selected label and native pose, and wait for the game's actual campsite benefit for each. Check repeated clicking does not execute twice. Reload, relog and restart to confirm pose/position/toggle persistence; settings are account-wide. Escape must close the pose chooser without dismissing another native tooltip.
6. Enter combat with the bar/flyout visible, during queued inventory work, and during a drag. Repeat with the chooser closed and with no carried camp objects. The secure bar and chooser must hide; the preview must also disappear, with no blocked action or Lua error, especially `RestrictedFrames.lua:84: Invalid frame handle`. Move away from camp during combat, then leave combat: stale actions must remain hidden. Return to camp: the fresh inventory must appear and the chooser must stay closed. Repeat after visiting a camp, walking away and entering combat with the bar already hidden. Disabling the feature in combat must clean up afterward; subsequent combat while disabled must remain error-free and never reveal the bar. A drag interrupted by combat must restore its last saved anchor. While disabled, confirm no camping timer or feature event registration remains.
7. At several UI scales, check the quiet slate/teal style, header text, pose chooser, item spacing, cooldowns and count layering. Test movement near screen edges. Plain right-click must keep the position; Ctrl-right-click must restore the default. Record screenshots and the running build before marking visuals verified.

## Macro smoke test (0.1.21)

Record `/wfs status` first; the running build and Interface are required with results. Disable overlapping macro management in other addons for this test, while retaining their existing macros.

1. `/reload`, then open `/wfs` and click the Macros sidebar entry. The preview, Create / update button and automatic-update control must all appear without the SetFont error. Navigate away and back, then close and reopen using `/wfs macros`. Check the sixth sidebar page, two-column recipe view, supply names, explanations and scrollable copyable preview at your UI scale. No macro should be created at login or by opening/selecting a recipe. Automatic updates and both combat checkboxes must start off on first use. X/Escape must close the panel, including when the preview has focus.
2. Carry vendor and conjured food/drink, buff food, a healing potion, mana potion and Healthstone. Check automatic food/drink prefer conjured supplies and omit buff food. Create FS Food and FS Drink. Open Blizzard's Macros window and drag both from General onto action bars. Press outside combat: only the corresponding food/drink should be attempted. Check the generated combat options separately: Food attempts Healthstone and healing potion in combat, and Drink attempts mana potion. Test with one supply missing and with each checkbox off. Native cooldowns and item rules decide whether both health items can be consumed; do not report this as a cooldown fallback.
3. Create FS Bandage and press it with another target selected; it should bandage yourself when native rules allow. Close Blizzard's Macros window while creating or updating through Suite. With an empty off hand, choose its automatic coating recipe and click Create placeholder. The General macro must exist and contain only `#showtooltip`; drag it to an action bar. With Keep created macros current on, equip a supported off-hand weapon and carry its matching stone: the existing macro/action-bar button must gain the correct item and slot 17 commands, without creating another macro. Remove the weapon or equip a shield: its use commands must clear. Repeat a main-hand placeholder with no matching supplies, then acquire supplies. Turn updates off and verify the placeholder stays inert until an explicit update or enabling updates. Carry ordinary sharpening and weightstones and swap a sword/dagger/polearm for a mace/staff/fist weapon. With updates on, inspect the generated/saved text after each swap outside combat. Test supply level requirements and missing matching stones. Choose an oil/poison, check the expected hand, apply it, then Select automatically to return to stone matching. Record any native confirmation prompts or unexpected targeting.
4. Add two item macros, choose distinct usable buffs and create each. Their names and choices must stay separate. Test the item's normal targeting, including any native targeting cursor. Remove one choice: its game macro must remain, and future bag changes must not update it. Check Previous/Next when more than six recipes exist. Scroll a long preview and copy it; typing must not change the generated recipe.
5. Enable Keep created macros current. Consume/remove supplies and receive new ones. Saved macros should follow complete bag data outside combat; confirmed empty automatic supply sets should clear obsolete actions. Turn updates off and close the page; bag changes must leave saved macro text alone. Uncreated recipes must never appear solely from events. Test a newly encountered uncached supply; existing text must remain while data is loading.
6. While Blizzard's Macros window is open, change supplies and type an unsaved edit. Suite must pause writes. Close the native editor: the saved external text edit must remain and Suite must explain that updates are paused for that macro. To restore a managed recipe while retaining that custom copy, rename the custom macro and click Create / update in Suite. Test a same-name unrelated General macro, duplicate names, deleting a created macro, full General slots and an existing character macro with the same name. No user/EverCore/character macro should be overwritten or deleted.
7. Enter combat with the page open, during a queued bag update and with a coating macro on an action bar. Create, item choice and combat-option editing must be unavailable; automatic macro edits must wait until combat ends. The native action-bar macro itself remains under game rules. Check for Lua errors and blocked actions.
8. Reload, relog and restart the client. Confirm chosen items, combat additions and item variants survive. As of 0.1.92, an alt recognizes existing confirmed Suite General macros automatically; with shared updates on, its choices/supplies update those macros without another Create. Missing/unowned/edited/duplicate names remain protected. Follow the new shared-macro checklist above for item imports and local removal persistence. Return to the first character and check the disclosed last-character behavior. Record running build, visual result, execution result and coexistence with EverCore separately.
